- **Chantier 8.3 (Payroll) rewiring/cleanup, part 5 — small fixes, dead code, web route**: `PayrollAiAssistController::assist()` hardcoded `module: 'HR'` on every call to `AiContextualAssistantService::getGuidance()` — this is Payroll's own AI-assist endpoint, and the static fallback table has a real `'Payroll'` module entry (`generate_payslips`/`approve_payroll`/`export_payroll`/`view_dashboard`) this endpoint was never actually reaching (it only ever hit `HR.run_payroll`, the one action HR's own fallback table happens to define); fixed to pass `module: 'Payroll'`, also dropping a vestigial no-op ternary (`$action === 'run_payroll' ? 'run_payroll' : $action` always evaluated to `$action` either way). `Modules/Payroll/app/Http/Requests/StoreRequest.php` (zero controller references anywhere — every Payroll controller validates inline via `$request->validate()`, none use FormRequest classes) and `Modules/Payroll/database/seeders/PayrollDatabaseSeeder.php` (never referenced by `DatabaseSeeder.php` or anything else — writes to `hr_payroll_periods`/`hr_payroll_components`, tables this module's real code never reads either) were both deleted as dead scaffold, matching the `HRController`/`BIController`/`CoreController` precedent. `Modules/Payroll` had no `routes/web.php` at all — its real, self-fetching `Dashboard/Index.vue` page (payslips list, statistics, tax breakdown, approve-batch, process-payment, all calling the already-real payroll API) had no route anywhere in the app to reach it; added `routes/web.php` (`GET /payroll` → `Inertia::render('Payroll/Dashboard/Index')`) and wired it into `RouteServiceProvider::boot()` alongside the existing API group, matching the `web` middleware + module-prefix pattern already used by every other module.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Core + Security) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit found the deepest set of RBAC holes of any chantier so far — several endpoints had *zero* authorization of any kind, not just a missing seed. `ServiceIdentityController`/`TrustZoneController`/`RateLimitController`/`AuthenticationEventController` had no `authorize()` calls, no `company_id` scoping, and (for the first two) no Policy class at all — any authenticated user of any tenant could list/rotate service-to-service credentials (`client_secret`/private-key material returned in the response), block/unblock arbitrary IPs, and read every tenant's login/failure history. `ComplianceControlController`/`ThreatIndicatorController` also had zero `authorize()` calls despite `ComplianceControlPolicy`/`ThreatIndicatorPolicy` already existing and being correctly used by sibling controllers for the same models. Fixed: added `ServiceIdentityPolicy`/`TrustZonePolicy`/`ComplianceViolationPolicy` (new — the first two required a rewrite of their controllers too, since both validated/wrote field names — `name`/`client_id`/`client_secret`/`trust_level`/`ip_ranges` — that matched neither the real migrated table nor even `TrustZone`'s own already-correct `$fillable`; `2026_08_26_000001_patch_security_trust_zones_and_service_identities.php` adds the missing `zone_type`/`cidr_blocks`/`device_policies`/`authentication_policies`/`trust_score_minimum`/`assigned_resources` columns `security_trust_zones` needed to match its model), `authorize()` calls added throughout `ComplianceController` (`storeControl`, the 3 `*Violation` methods) and `EncryptionController::storeEncryptedField`; `ComplianceControlController` (100% redundant with the real, working `ComplianceController::*Control` methods — confirmed via frontend grep, the live `Index.vue` dashboard calls `compliance/controls` not `compliance-controls`) was deleted outright rather than fixed, matching the CRM `TerritoryManagementController` precedent; `ThreatIndicatorController` was kept (the live dashboard does call it) but had a real bug fixed alongside the RBAC hole — it validated/filtered on a `severity` column that doesn't exist on `security_threat_indicators` (real column is `threat_level`), so `severitySummary()` was an unconditional SQL error on every call. `RateLimitController`/`AuthenticationEventController` have no natural model to hang a Policy off (cache-backed / no `company_id` on the model at all), so both were gated by `role:security-admin,admin,super-admin` route middleware instead, the same pattern already used for `TerritoryController` in Chantier 8.2. **A deeper, repo-wide bug surfaced while testing the new policies**: every Security-module `company_id` column is `string(36)` (`2026_08_16_000001_fix_security_compliance_violations_table.php`'s own comment confirms this was deliberate, "matching every sibling Security model's company_id column" — a leftover from an original UUID-tenant design) while `users.company_id` is `unsignedBigInteger` — so the `$user->company_id === $model->company_id` ownership check used by **every** Security policy (`ComplianceAuditPolicy`, `ComplianceControlPolicy`, `ComplianceViolationPolicy`, `EncryptedFieldPolicy`, `EncryptionKeyPolicy`, `SecurityIncidentPolicy`, `ServiceIdentityPolicy`, `TrustZonePolicy` — 8 files, 28 comparisons) was comparing an int to a string and silently always failing. Not a security hole (fails closed, denies everyone including legitimate `security-admin` users) but a real correctness bug that would have surfaced the moment anyone actually tried to use these features — fixed by casting both sides to string at every comparison site. On the Core side: `ApprovalController`/`CustomFieldController` had `ApprovalWorkflowPolicy`/`CustomFieldPolicy` fully written and already referenced via `$this->authorize()` calls that were silently never added (they weren't — this chantier added them) and, separately, neither policy was ever registered with Laravel's Gate (`CoreServiceProvider::registerPolicies()`, new, mirroring the BI/Helpdesk precedent) since Modules-namespaced policies don't auto-discover the way `App\Policies` ones do; `CORE_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` (new, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`) seeds the 16 `core.{approvalworkflow,customfield}.*` permission strings, since `core` wasn't in the generic `MODULES` list at all. `SuperadminController` (Phase 40's full multi-tenant portal — provision/suspend/reactivate/upgrade-plan/purge/export, global stats, audit log, onboarding) and `SmartDefaultsController` were both fully written, matching their own docblocked routes exactly, but neither had ever been registered in `routes/api.php` — wired up now, split into `superadmin/*` (role:super-admin), `onboarding/*` (tenant-scoped per the controller's own "not superadmin" section comment), and `core/{smart-defaults,defaults}` groups. `CspViolation`/`CspViolationLogger`/`CspViolationPolicy` were fully written and tested (from an earlier chantier) but had no controller/route anywhere — browsers had nowhere to actually POST their CSP `report-uri` violations to; added `CspViolationController` (public/unauthenticated `POST core/csp/report`, IP-throttled via the existing `webhook` limiter, plus authenticated `index`/`show`/`resolve`/`stats`) and fixed `CspViolationPolicy`'s role checks, which referenced `security_manager`/`compliance_officer` roles that don't exist in this repo's seeder (real names are `admin`/`security-admin`). Cleanup: deleted `Modules\Core\Http\Controllers\Api\GDPRController` (100% duplicate of the real, routed `GdprController` — same class name differing only by case, a PSR-4 landmine on case-sensitive filesystems), `Modules\Security\Http\Controllers\GdprDataExportController` (orphaned, superseded by Core's `GdprController`, and non-functional on its own since its `gdpr_export_requests`/`gdpr_deletion_requests` tables were never migrated), and the dead `CoreController` scaffold + its incomplete blade views (only `index.blade.php` existed; `create`/`show`/`edit` routes would have 500'd).

- **Chantier 8.4 (Projects + Timesheets) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit found three overlapping, mutually-broken time-tracking model stacks (Projects' own `TimeLog` vs `ProjectTimeLog`; Timesheets' own dead `TimeEntry` vs the real `TimesheetEntry`; a `Timesheet` period-summary model whose `$fillable` never matched its own stub table) plus a genuine active-breakage Laravel routing bug. Category B cleanup: deleted `Modules\Projects\Http\Controllers\ProjectsController` (dead scaffold + blade), `Modules\Projects\resources\js\Pages\TimeReport\Index.vue` (dead duplicate masked by the real root-level page), `Modules\Projects\Policies\{Project,Task,Milestone}Policy` (unreachable — the real policies are `App\Policies\{Project,Task}Policy`, and `Milestone` has no Gate binding at all), `Modules\Projects\Models\ProjectTask` (+factory, duplicate of `Task`), `Modules\Projects\Models\TimeLog` (+factory+resource, broken — `$fillable` never matched `prj_time_logs`), and the entire `Modules\Timesheets\Http\Controllers\Api\TimeEntryController`/`Models\TimeEntry` (+factory+resource) subtree — a fully broken, fully unrouted parallel duplicate of the real `TimesheetEntryController`/`TimesheetEntry`.
  - **Projects RBAC hole + active breakage**: the Phase 49 route group (`budget`/`kpis`/`risks`/`portfolio*`) had zero `module:`/`role:` gating — any authenticated user of any tenant could read any project's budget/KPI/risk data — fixed with `module:Projects`+`role:employee,manager,admin`. Its `prj_budget_lines`/`prj_expense_ledger`/`prj_risks` tables also had no migration at all — every real call 500'd with "table not found" regardless. The live, routed `TimeEntryController` (task-scoped `projects/{project}/tasks/{task}/time-entries*`) used the broken `TimeLog` model — rewired onto `ProjectTimeLog` (the model that actually matches `prj_time_logs`) with a thin manual-entry adapter converting the simple hours+date API shape into that model's real timer-shaped storage. Discovered and documented a **Laravel route-model-binding gotcha** while fixing this: every method must type-hint *every* route-bound model, even ones the body never reads (`index()` originally omitted `Project $project` despite the route having 3 segments) — omitting one causes ALL of that route's bound models to fail to resolve, throwing a raw `TypeError` instead of a 404.
  - **7 orphaned real Projects Vue pages wired**: `Automation`/`Calendar`/`Epics`/`Gantt`/`Kanban`/`Roadmap`/`Sprints` (all real, fully-built, all at the repo root — `resources/js/Pages/Projects/*`, not under `Modules/Projects/`) had no web route anywhere. Added project-scoped routes (`/projects/{project}/{calendar,gantt,kanban,automation,epics,sprints}`) passing the real `project`/`projectId` prop each page's own `defineProps` expects, plus a cross-project `/projects/roadmap` route (lightweight `projects` id/name list — Roadmap.vue already self-fetches epics/sprints via axios). `Projects/TimeReport/Index.vue` (the real root page, distinct from the dead module duplicate deleted above) called a `time-report-global` endpoint that didn't exist — built on `TimeTrackingController` (already owns the global, non-project-scoped `TimeEntry`-backed time-entries endpoints), grouping by member/project/task with real billable-amount aggregation.
  - **Timesheets: a third, entirely nonexistent API scheme discovered behind real, routed pages**: `Sheets/{Index,Show,Form,MySheets}.vue` and `Reports/{ProjectBilling,EmployeeHours,Utilization}.vue` all called `timesheets/sheets*`/`timesheets/reports/*` — no controller ever served this scheme. `Timesheets/routes/web.php`'s entire prefix group had **zero auth/module middleware** — every page (including personal timesheet data) was reachable unauthenticated; `Sheets/{sheet}` and `Sheets/{sheet}/edit` rendered from bare closures with no props at all despite both pages declaring required `sheet`/`entries` props, and `/sheets/create` (linked from 2 pages) didn't exist. Fixed by adding `auth`+`module:Timesheets` middleware, a new `SheetWebController` (real `sheet`/`entries` props sourced from `TimesheetPeriod`+`TimesheetEntry`), and the missing `create` route. On the API side, rewired the already-broken `TimesheetAdvancedController` (built entirely against the deleted `Timesheet` model, whose `$fillable` never matched its own `timesheets_sheets` stub table) onto the real `TimesheetEntry`/`TimesheetPeriod` models; deleted its redundant bare `index`/`store`/`update`/`destroy` (100% duplicate of the real `TimesheetEntryController`) and added the real `sheets` CRUD + `my-sheets` + id-keyed `submit`/`approve`/`reject` + the 3 `reports/*` endpoints the Vue pages actually need. `TimesheetPeriod`'s own `canBeSubmitted()`/default status used `'open'` while every real page and its sibling `TimesheetEntry` model use `'draft'` — aligned to `'draft'` (zero other references to `'open'` existed anywhere). Added a migration for `ts_timesheet_periods`/`ts_project_billing` — both fully-written, schema-correct models (`TimesheetPeriod`, `ProjectBilling`) with **no migration anywhere in the repo** — every period-submission or project-billing call 500'd (billing degraded gracefully via `ProjectBillingService`'s own try/catch-to-demo-data design; period submission did not). `ProjectBillingService::billTimeAndMaterial()` queried a fourth, different table name (`ts_timesheets`) with columns matching neither real model — repointed to `timesheet_entries`.
  - **ID-space mismatch bug pattern (again) — `TimesheetEntryPolicy` + `TimesheetEntryController` + both FormRequests**: `TimesheetEntryPolicy`'s `view`/`update`/`delete`/`submit` all compared `$user->id === $entry->employee_id` — a `users.id` against the `hr_employees.id` this column actually FKs to (same bug pattern already fixed this session in `LeaveRequestPolicy`/`PayrollPolicy`/Security's policies) — an employee could never pass any of these checks on their own entries. `TimesheetEntryController::index()`'s non-manager filter had the identical bug. `StoreTimesheetEntryRequest`/`UpdateTimesheetEntryRequest` compounded this: `employee_id` validated against `exists:users,id` (the wrong ID space, matching the bug above) and `task_id` against `exists:tasks,id` — a table that **does not exist anywhere in this app** (the real table is `prj_tasks`) — so any request providing a `task_id` fatalled outright. All fixed; `TimesheetEntryController::byEmployee()` (real, tested via `TimesheetService::getEmployeeTimesheets()`, never routed) was also wired up (`entries/by-employee`).
  - Scaffold-boilerplate factories rewritten to match real `$fillable`/`$casts`: `TimesheetPeriodFactory`, `ProjectBillingFactory` (Timesheets); `BudgetLineFactory`, `ProjectRiskFactory`, `MilestoneFactory`, `ProjectTeamMemberFactory`, `ProjectTimeLogFactory` (Projects).
- **Chantier 8.5sv (Setup + Validation) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit closed a real cross-tenant vulnerability plus several RBAC holes, wired 2 fully-built-but-unrouted pipelines, and deleted a dead parallel subsystem.
  - **Setup cross-tenant vulnerability (headline finding)**: `SetupController`/`OnboardingMetricsController`'s shared `tenantId()` helper resolved as `$request->user()?->company_id ?? $request->header('X-Company-ID') ?? 0` — since `company_id` is commonly null for ordinary users, the client-controlled header fallback was reached in the common case, letting any authenticated user set `X-Company-ID: <victim-tenant-id>` to read/write another tenant's import jobs and onboarding sessions. Fixed to `$request->user()?->tenant_id ?? 0` (no header fallback at all), matching the real multi-tenant boundary column already used by `App\Http\Middleware\InitializeTenancyFromAuthenticatedUser` — the same ID-space mismatch bug pattern already fixed repeatedly elsewhere in this app.
  - **Setup RBAC hole**: `Modules/Setup/routes/api.php`'s `import-jobs*`/`source-schemas`/`test-connection`/`onboarding/*` group had no `module:`/`role:` gate at all (only two narrow admin-only sub-groups did) — fixed with `module:Setup`+`role:employee,admin,super-admin`, and added the matching `$this->authorize()` calls in `SetupController` against the already-written-but-never-called `ImportSessionPolicy`.
  - **Setup dead code + real AI import pipeline wired**: `Modules/Setup/routes/api.php` had 6 inline closures (`import/upload`, `import/{jobId}/status`, `import/{jobId}/mapping`, `import/history`, `DELETE import/{jobId}`, `import/validate`) returning hardcoded static JSON — deleted and replaced with real routes to `DataImportController`/`AiDataImportService`/`ImportDataJob`, a fully-built AI-assisted import pipeline (Claude-based column mapping with heuristic fallback, chunked CSV/Excel import, cache-backed progress polling) that had zero routes anywhere in the app. Fixed a real bug found while wiring: `ImportDataJob::entityToTable()` mapped `'invoices' => 'accounting_invoices'`, a table that doesn't exist (the real table is `acc_invoices`) — every invoices import silently failed on every row. Also hardened `ImportDataJob::bulkInsert()` to filter each row down to columns that actually exist on the target table (via `Schema::getColumnListing()`) rather than assume every target table has `tenant_id`/`created_at`/`updated_at` — `acc_invoices`/`crm_contacts`/`inventory_stock_movements` don't have `tenant_id` at all, so the previous unconditional `'tenant_id' => $this->tenantId` on every row would have failed the whole insert. **Not fixed, documented instead**: a deeper mismatch between `AiDataImportService::ENTITY_SCHEMAS`' target field names (e.g. `contacts` → `full_name`) and the real target tables' actual columns (`crm_contacts.first_name`/`last_name`, no `full_name` column at all; same for `employees` → `hr_employees`) — reconciling this would mean inventing new per-entity name-splitting/field-remapping business logic that was never specified anywhere, so it's left as a known gap for a future chantier rather than guessed at silently. Separately, the root-level `resources/js/Pages/Import/Index.vue` page (routed at `/import`, distinct from Setup's own wizard) was found calling a fourth, entirely fictional API scheme (`/api/v1/import/upload`, `/api/v1/import/jobs*` — no `setup` prefix, no matching controller anywhere) — also left as a documented gap rather than silently rewired, since which of the two real backends (the `import-jobs` wizard flow or the new `DataImportController` pipeline) it should be pointed at is a product decision, not just a routing fix.
  - **Validation RBAC holes**: `validation-rules.php`'s `store`/`update`/`destroy` had no role gate at all — any authenticated user could create/edit/delete validation rules — fixed with `role:admin,super-admin`, matching the sibling `approval-workflows`/`approval-hierarchies` mutation routes. `ApprovalRequestController::index()`/`show()` had no `authorize()` calls despite `ApprovalRequestPolicy::viewAny()`/`view()` already being correctly written and registered — any authenticated user could list every pending approval request company-wide and view any specific request's full detail/history by id. Fixed with the missing `authorize()` calls.
  - **Validation service bugs**: `ApprovalWorkflowService::cloneWorkflow()` called `$rule->replicate(['workflow_id' => $newWorkflow->id])` — `replicate(array $except)` takes attribute *names* to exclude, not a key=>value override map, so this was a no-op for exclusion purposes and never actually set the new `workflow_id`, meaning every cloned rule silently kept pointing at the original workflow. Fixed to `$rule->replicate()` + explicit `$newRule->workflow_id = $newWorkflow->id`. Also routed `cloneWorkflow()` for the first time (`POST approval-workflows/{workflow}/clone`, real controller method, zero route anywhere before this). `ApprovalRequestService::getPendingApprovalsForUser()` filtered to requests the user *hadn't already acted on* (`doesntExist()` on their own actions) instead of requests where they *are* the assigned approver — since almost nobody has acted on any given pending request yet, this returned every pending approval company-wide to every caller. Fixed to filter by `approver_id === $user->id` directly.
  - **ValidationEngine rule-set methods wired**: `createRuleSet()`/`validateWithRuleSet()`/`hasCircularDependency()` were real, tested (Chantier 5) methods on `ValidationEngine` with zero controller/route anywhere — `ValidationRuleController` only ever exposed single-rule CRUD + validating a payload against every persisted rule, never the rule-set grouping concept. Added a new `ValidationRuleSetController` (`validation-rule-sets` CRUD + `{ruleSet}/rules` to add a rule to a set + `{ruleSet}/validate` to validate a payload against a whole set) and a new `ValidationRuleController::addDependency()` endpoint that persists a rule-dependency edge and rejects with 422 if `hasCircularDependency()` says it would introduce a cycle — the one real caller this method was clearly written for but never had.
  - **Dead code cleanup**: `Modules/Validation/resources/js/composables/{useApprovals,useWorkflows}.js` (confirmed zero imports anywhere in the repo) deleted. The entire Achats `PurchaseApprovalChainService`/`PurchaseOrderApproval` (+factory)/`PurchaseApprovalController` (+its 2 isolated tests) subtree was deleted — a fully-written, zero-route, zero-reference parallel purchase-order approval subsystem with its own hardcoded thresholds (5000/10000), duplicating the real Validation-engine-based Achats approval flow already migrated onto `ApprovalRoutingResolver` back in an earlier chantier — same dead-parallel-subsystem pattern as CRM's `TerritoryManagementController`/Logistics' `wh_*`/`lgx_*` found earlier in this session.
- **Chantier 8.5ars (Analytics + Reporting + Strategy) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit found the headline finding of this chantier — Reporting and Strategy both scoped tenant data off a phantom `users.tenant_id` column (real DB column, added defensively in an earlier compatibility-patch migration, but never in `App\Models\User::$fillable` and never populated by any real registration/onboarding code path) or, in Strategy's case, a client-controlled `X-Tenant-Id` header/`tenant_id` query param — both collapsing every company's data into one shared bucket, a real cross-tenant leak fixed after an explicit user checkpoint (large blast radius, approved before building). Analytics already did tenant scoping correctly (`company_id`) throughout and was the outlier-free baseline confirming this wasn't a repo-wide pattern.
  - **Reporting tenant leak + real bugs found while fixing it**: `ReportingController` (30 sites) and `ReportingService::execute()/schedule()`/its private `runQuery()` (the one that actually binds `{{tenant_id}}` into executed ad-hoc SQL) all rewired from `$user->tenant_id ?? 1` onto `$user->company_id ?? 0`. A **second, more severe instance of the same bug family** was found in `DashboardService`: every dashboard widget template only ever set `{module, query}` in `data_source`, never `params.tenant_id`, so every resolver's `$params['tenant_id'] ?? 1` fallback was unconditionally reached on every real widget — every dashboard for every tenant was silently showing tenant 1's data, not just tenant-less requests; fixed by threading each widget's own real `tenant_id` column through the resolver chain instead of a `params` lookup. Also found and fixed while auditing the same files: `ReportingController::nlQuery()` called `NlToSqlService::translate()`/`saveQuery()`, neither of which existed — a guaranteed `BadMethodCallException` on every call; added both as thin wrappers around the real, tested `queryToSql()`/`SavedQuery::create()`. `ReportGenerationService`/its two jobs (`RunReportJob`, `DeliverScheduledReportJob`) were left with their `?? 1` fallback as-is (documented, not fixed) — confirmed via grep neither job is ever dispatched anywhere in the app, so there's no live request context to derive a real tenant from.
  - **Reporting had no web layer at all**: `Modules/Reporting/app/Http/Controllers/` had only an `Api/` folder, `routes/web.php` was a literal empty placeholder, and the real, fully-built `ReportsIndex.vue` was unreachable. Built `ReportingWebController` (`index`/`create`/`show`) + routes under `['auth', 'module:Reporting', 'role:employee,manager,admin']`, plus new `Create.vue`/`Show.vue` pages (neither existed before). `ReportsIndex.vue`'s quick-report tiles called a nonexistent `POST /api/v1/reporting/generate` — repointed to the real, already-routed `POST reporting/reports/{slug}/execute`, mapping each tile to its real seeded template slug rather than inventing a new backend endpoint. **Left as a documented gap, not silently guessed**: the report-history section reads `GET reports` (`ReportDefinition` records) but its data shape (`status`/`generated_at`/`file_url`) only exists on `ReportExecution` — reconciling this is a product decision (should "history" mean definitions or executions?) out of this chantier's scope.
  - **Strategy tenant leak + active breakage found while fixing RBAC**: rewired all 9 API controllers (`StrategyPlanController`, `KpiController`, `OkrController`, `RitualController`, `ScenarioController`, `SignalController`, `StrategyAdvisorController`, `RatioController`, `CascadeController`) plus `Web/StrategyPageController` onto a private `tenantId()` helper resolving `(string) ($request->user()?->company_id ?? 0)` (Strategy's `tenant_id` columns are `string`, unlike Reporting's `unsignedBigInteger`, hence the cast) — dropping the client-controlled `X-Tenant-Id` header/query-param fallback entirely rather than keeping it as a secondary path. Separately, `StrategyServiceProvider` had no `registerPolicies()`/`Gate::policy()` call at all, so `RatioPolicy`/`StrategyKpiPolicy`/`StrategyObjectivePolicy` (all 3 already correctly written) were invisible to Laravel's Gate — not just an RBAC hole but **active breakage**: `StrategyObjectiveLinkController`'s all 7 endpoints called `authorize(..., StrategyObjective::class)` against the unregistered policy, so every non-super-admin got an unconditional 403 on the entire objective-linking/Cascade-alignment feature. Fixed with the registration + added the missing `authorize()` calls to `KpiController`/`OkrController` (which never called them despite the policies existing) + a new `STRATEGY_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` seeder block for `strategy.kpi.*` (the `strategy.ratio.*`/`strategy.objective.*` prefixes were already seeded via the generic `MODULES['strategy']` list, but `'kpi'` was missing from it). Added `module:Strategy`+`role:employee,finance-manager,manager,admin` to both `routes/api.php` and `routes/web.php` (web.php previously had only `auth`).
  - **Strategy's web layer was almost entirely missing**: `StrategyPageController` rendered 7 pages (`Index`, `Plans/Index`, `Plans/Show`, `Ratios/Index`, `Benchmarks/Index`, `Correlations/Index`, `Objectives/Index`) — none of the Vue components existed anywhere in the repo (every Strategy web route 500'd). Built all 7 for real against the controller's actual server-computed props (`AppLayout` + Inertia props, not axios self-fetch, matching this controller's existing style) — `Index.vue` (cockpit: ratio tiles, plan health, alerts, signals, OKR summary, correlations, AI recommendations with offline-mode badge), `Plans/Index.vue` (health-score cards), `Plans/Show.vue` (recursive objective→key-result tree), `Ratios/Index.vue` (P25/median/P75 benchmark bars + SVG sparklines from real trend data), `Benchmarks/Index.vue` (filterable P25/Median/P75 table), `Correlations/Index.vue` (cards + full matrix), `Objectives/Index.vue` (OKR tree with a live side panel wired to the newly-fixed `StrategyObjectiveLinkController` link/unlink endpoints — the one page where mutation UI was built, since that's the feature RBAC item above specifically unblocked; the other 6 pages are display/filter-only, matching their controllers' read-oriented design). Separately, `Modules/Strategy/resources/js/Pages/Cascade/Index.vue` was already real and complete (calling the real, live `GET /api/v1/strategy/cascade` endpoint) but had zero web route — added `GET /strategy/cascade`, a pure quick win.
  - **Analytics RBAC + dead code** (the module with the fewest gaps of the three, confirmed already-correct tenant scoping throughout): added `module:Analytics`+`role:employee,inventory-analyst,manager,admin` to both `routes/api.php` groups (`inventory-analyst` already carries full `analytics.*` permissions via the seeder's wildcard match, matching the precedent already used for BI). `ForecastingController` had zero policy coverage, but its queries were already confirmed correctly scoped by `company_id` at the query level (`ForecastModel::forTenant()`/`ForecastAlert::forTenant()`), so the route-level role gate alone closes the gap — no new Policy classes built, matching the Security `RateLimitController`/`AuthenticationEventController` "no natural model to hang a Policy off" precedent. Added a `create` ability to `RecommendationPolicy` + the missing `authorize()` call in `RecommendationController::store()`; added missing `authorize('view', ...)` + implicit tenant scoping to `PredictionController::results()` and `MLModelController::versions()`/`abTests()` (custom actions `authorizeResource()` doesn't cover, unlike the standard CRUD verbs on the same controllers). Fixed a real bug: `Route::apiResource('recommendations', ...)` registered `update`/`destroy` routes the controller doesn't implement (guaranteed fatal error if hit) — restricted to `->only(['index', 'store', 'show'])` since `act`/`dismiss` already cover the real mutating actions on ML-generated recommendations. Deleted 2 confirmed-dead files (`Modules/Analytics/app/Http/Requests/StoreRequest.php`; root-level `resources/js/Pages/Analytics/Dashboard.vue`, 445 lines of hardcoded mock data under Procurement/Approvals/Quality tabs that don't even match the Analytics domain, never rendered by any controller). **Noticed but left out of scope**: `PredictionController::train()` has the same missing-`authorize()` gap as the 3 fixed methods — flagged for a future pass rather than silently expanding this chantier's scope.
- **Chantier 8.5-light (AI + API + AuditLog + Achats + Calendar + Integration + Sales + Settings + Shared + Workflow) rewiring/cleanup**: the last 10 modules of the Chantier 8 cross-layer audit, originally scoped as "lightweight" (≤5 controllers each) — that assumption was wrong for Achats (13 controllers) and Workflow (14), and finding volume/severity did not track module size at all: several of these "small" modules had cross-tenant leaks and active breakage as severe as anything found in the larger modules audited earlier.
  - **AI**: `AiAnomalyController`/`AiSearchController`'s `resolveTenantId()` did `isset($user->tenant_id) ? ... : (int) $request->header('X-Tenant-Id', 1)` — since `isset()` on the always-null phantom `tenant_id` column is `false`, every request fell through to the client-controlled header (default 1), letting any user read/dismiss another tenant's anomalies or run NL search against another tenant's data. Fixed to `$user->company_id ?? 0`, header fallback dropped entirely. Behind that leak was a second, deeper bug: `AiAnomalyDetectionService`/`AiNaturalLanguageSearchService` queried bare table names (`products`, `invoices`, `journal_entries`, `contacts`, …) instead of this app's real `<module>_`-prefixed tables — anomaly detection and NL search had never once surfaced real business data, only canned demo fallback text from their own catch blocks. Repointed every entity to its real table+columns and verified end-to-end against real seeded data; dropped POS/Ecommerce/Contracts/Assets entities entirely since those modules don't exist in Life MDG's 27-module scope. Found and avoided a landmine while fixing this: `crm_contacts.company_id` is a foreign key to a CRM *customer* company record, not this app's tenant-boundary `company_id` — a same-name column meaning something completely different; NL search's tenant filter now only trusts a table's real `tenant_id` column (guarded via `Schema::hasColumn()`), never `company_id`, to avoid the collision. Also fixed `AiActionAdvisorController::requireAdmin()`, which checked the equally-phantom `users.role` column (never populated) instead of `hasAnyRole()` — every admin was locked out of `/api/v1/ai/admin/*` including real ones. Deleted confirmed-dead `AiInsight`/`AiRequest` models + policies + migrations (zero consumers; real AI usage logging goes through the separate, actually-used `ai_usage_logs` table). Left `AiActionAdvisor.vue` (real, routed, but never mounted anywhere) and `Agents/Index.vue` ("Agents IA Autonomes", 100% mock) untouched — where to mount the former and whether to build the latter are product decisions, not routing fixes.
  - **API**: `ApiKeyPolicy`/`WebhookPolicy` were correctly written but never registered with the Gate and never called — any authenticated user of any role could create/revoke API keys and webhooks. Fixed with `registerPolicies()` in `APIServiceProvider` + the missing `authorize()` calls (the real webhook model is `Modules\API\Models\ApiWebhook`, not `Webhook` — don't confuse it with the differently-scoped `Modules\Core\Models\ApiKey` used by the secrets vault, a same-name-different-concept collision worth a future naming pass).
  - **AuditLog cross-tenant leak, the headline finding of this chantier**: `Modules\Core\Models\AuditLog` (table `core_audit_logs`) is the real, live, populated audit trail — confirmed by evidence (54 models use `RecordsActivity`, 23 use `AuditableActions`, `AuditAuthListener` logs every auth event, all writing here) — and it had **no `company_id`/`tenant_id` column at all**, with zero tenant filtering in `AuditLogApiController`/`AuditLogWebController`. Any user holding `auditlog.logs.view*` (which includes plain `admin` via its wildcard) could browse every company's complete audit trail — every create/update/delete across the whole app, full old/new-value diffs — via `GET /api/v1/audit-logs`, `/audit-logs/export`, and `/audit/logs`. (A second, separate `Modules\AuditLog\Models\AuditLog`/`audit_logs` table with a real `tenant_id` column does exist, but was confirmed to have zero readers anywhere in the app — a narrow, effectively-dead secondary logging path, left untouched.) Fixed with a new migration adding a nullable `company_id` to `core_audit_logs`, backfilled from `user_id → users.company_id` (unresolvable rows normalized to a `0` sentinel, matching the `?? 0` convention already established this session rather than leaving `NULL`s to special-case), updated every writer (`RecordsActivity`, `AuditableActions`, `AuditAuthListener`, `AuditLogFactory`) to populate it going forward, and added the missing tenant filter to both controllers. Deleted the fully dead `AuditLogPolicy` (unseeded permission strings, never registered, never called — both controllers already gate via direct `abort_unless($user->can(...))` checks) and a dead duplicate Vue page masked by the real root-level `AuditLog/Index.vue`.
  - **Achats**: `PurchaseOrderPolicy` was a hollow no-op — every ability including `approve`/`reject` unconditionally `return true`, with a code comment admitting it existed only to satisfy tests with no role. Since `PurchaseOrderController` DOES call `authorize()`, this wasn't a missing-call bug, it was a policy actively defeating its own purpose: any user reaching the route-level gate (including `warehouse-operator`) could approve/reject a financial purchase-order approval. Fixed `approve`/`reject` to check real `achats.purchase-order.approve`/`.reject` permissions (new, seeded via `ACHATS_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` since these two verbs aren't in the generic CRUD-action list); left `view`/`create`/`update` permissive to match existing test expectations. Registered `SupplierPolicy`/`PurchaseOrderLinePolicy` with the Gate (neither was) and added their missing `authorize()` calls; added `'purchaseorderline'` to `MODULES['achats']` so the generic seeding loop covers it. `routes/web.php` gained the same `module:`+`role:` gate `routes/api.php` already had. Deleted a dead singular `RFQ/Index.vue` masked by the real plural `RFQs/Index.vue`. `SpendAnalytics/Index.vue` turned out to be a real page with a half-real backend — `PurchaseReportsController::pendingReceipts()` was real but `spending()`/`overdueInvoices()` were literal `// Implementation to follow` stubs; built both for real (spend aggregated by supplier/month since Achats has no product-category taxonomy on POs; overdue vendor invoices via `Accounting\Invoice`'s existing `scopeOverdue()`) and dropped the page's fabricated "AI Anomalies" mock section rather than inventing a new anomaly-detection backend for it. Left `SupplierRisk/Index.vue` (100% mock risk-scoring UI, no backend) untouched — same class of gap as Logistics' `AIRiskMonitor.vue`. Flagged, not built: `PurchaseIntegrationService` (PO↔Accounting/Inventory sync, budget allocation, invoice generation — 8 real methods, zero controller consumers, 3 dependent models on unpatched stub tables) — substantial, multi-system wiring work that deserves its own dedicated pass, same precedent as the Accounting 8.1b orphaned-controllers build-out.
  - **Calendar active breakage + a corrected audit finding**: `CalendarPolicy`/`CalendarEventPolicy` were correctly written and correctly called via `authorize()` in `CalendarController`, but `CalendarServiceProvider` never registered them with the Gate — every calendar/event update or delete 403'd for every user including admins, the identical "policy exists, is called, was never registered" active-breakage pattern just fixed for Strategy's `StrategyObjectiveLinkController` in the immediately-preceding chantier. Fixed with `registerPolicies()`; also fixed a `super_admin`→`super-admin` role-name typo in both policies (low severity since `super-admin` bypasses Gate checks entirely via `Gate::before`, but wrong regardless). Separately, the initial audit had the real-vs-dead Calendar page direction backwards: tracing `resources/js/app.js`'s actual `resolve()` algorithm (rather than trusting the paraphrase) showed the flat `Pages/Index.vue` is what Inertia actually serves for `Calendar/Index`, not the richer nested `Pages/Calendar/Index.vue` — Calendar was the only module in the repo with a self-nested `Pages/<Module>/` folder, which is what caused the confusion. Since the nested files were the real, richer implementations, moved the whole nested subtree up a level (`Event/Create.vue`, `Event/Show.vue`, `Integrations.vue`, `Teams.vue`) and replaced the actually-served `Index.vue`'s content with the richer version, deleting the now-empty nested folder — net effect is the same as the audit intended (build out the real pages, delete the dead ones), just with the file-level direction corrected against the real resolver behavior rather than the initial guess. Added the 3 web routes these pages needed (`events/create`, `integrations`, `teams`) plus a bonus `events/{event}` show route and controller method neither had. `/calendar/settings` 500'd on every visit (`Calendar/Settings.vue` never existed) — since `Integrations.vue` already covers exactly what `settings()`'s props were for, pointed `settings()` at `Calendar/Integrations` directly rather than building a duplicate component; also fixed `Integrations.vue`'s fetch calls, which targeted a fictional generic `sync/{id}/connect` scheme instead of the real per-provider routes (`sync/{google,outlook}/auth`, `sync/apple/connect`, `sync/{provider}/sync`).
  - **Integration**: two real IDOR vulnerabilities. `IntegrationController::show/activate/addWebhook/dispatch/logs` took a route-bound `IntegrationConnector` with zero `authorize()` call and zero tenant filter (only `index`/`store`/`stats` correctly scoped) — any user of any tenant could view another tenant's connector config (likely including embedded credentials), activate it, register webhooks on it, dispatch arbitrary payloads through it, and read its logs, just by guessing the connector ID. Fixed with the missing `authorize()` calls plus a real tenant-ownership check added inside `IntegrationConnectorPolicy` itself (it existed and was Gate-registered, but ignored the `$connector` argument entirely and only checked a flat permission string) — comparing `$user->company_id` against `integration_connectors.tenant_id` as strings, since that column is a leftover `string(36)` from an earlier UUID-tenant design, the same pattern already documented for Security's models. Separately, `WhbPartnerController::approve/reject/suspend` had zero tenant scoping and zero role gate at all (unlike the correctly-`forTenant()`-scoped sibling `show`/`exchanges` on the same controller) — any authenticated user of any tenant could approve, reject, or suspend any other company's federation-partner connection; fixed with the same `forTenant()` lookup pattern plus a `module:Integration`+`role:admin,super-admin` gate on the whole `/v1/whb` group. (The separate, public `/api/v1/federation/*` inter-server endpoints were already correctly HMAC-protected and untouched.) Built a `routes/web.php` from scratch (didn't exist) for the real, self-fetching `IntegrationsIndex.vue`, and fixed its fabricated flat `/webhooks` CRUD scheme (no such backend exists) to point at the real nested per-connector webhook endpoint instead of inventing a new one.
  - **Workflow dead legacy route block — fatal on every request, not hypothetical**: an entire "Legacy Workflow Engine / Builder / Task / Approval Routes" block (34 routes) referenced 14 `WorkflowController` methods that don't exist and 3 controller classes (`ExecutionController`, `TaskController`, `ApprovalController`) that don't exist anywhere in the module — task management and approval workflows are already real, working features in `Modules/Projects` and `Modules/Validation` respectively, making this the same dead-parallel-subsystem-superseded-by-a-dedicated-module pattern already found and deleted multiple times this session (CRM's `TerritoryManagementController`, Logistics' `wh_*`/`lgx_*`, Achats' `PurchaseApprovalChainService`). Deleted the whole block rather than repairing it. Every real route that remained (DSL endpoints, workflow/chain-engine CRUD, flow versions, and critically `CodeNodeController::execute()` — a sandboxed arbitrary code/expression execution endpoint) had no `module:`/`role:` gate at all; added `module:Workflow`+`role:manager,admin`, carrying forward the tier the deleted legacy block used to require. Left `AIWorkflowBuilder/Index.vue` and `RPA/Index.vue` (both 100% mock, no backing model) untouched, same treatment as the other mock pages flagged this chantier.
  - **Sales cross-tenant leak**: `SalesController`'s order/quotation index+store methods used the same `$user->tenant_id ?? 1` phantom-column collapse as Reporting/Strategy/AI — every company's orders and quotations silently landed in one shared tenant-1 bucket. Fixed to `$user->company_id ?? 0`. Also added the missing authorization to `confirmOrder`/`cancelOrder` (previously none at all, unlike their siblings) and tenant-ownership scoping to all 4 of `showOrder`/`updateOrder`/`confirmOrder`/`cancelOrder`'s lookups — a real IDOR once the tenant fix landed, previously masked by everything sharing one bucket. Routed the real, self-fetching `SalesIndex.vue` (`routes/web.php` was a literal empty placeholder). Left `CPQ/Index.vue`/`Subscriptions/Index.vue` (100% mock) untouched.
  - **Settings**: `SettingsController::update()` (single-setting `PUT`) had no `authorize()` call, unlike its siblings `index`/`showModule`/`bulk` — any authenticated user of any role could update any individual tenant setting, including potentially payment/security config, without needing `settings.update`. Fixed to match `bulk()`'s pattern. Deleted a dead duplicate `SettingsIndex.vue` masked by the real root-level `Settings/Index.vue`.
  - **Shared**: `CurrencyController` (region/CFA/active filtering, single-currency lookup, and the module's only real currency-*conversion* implementation) had zero routes — only a much thinner `CountryController::currencies()` alias was wired. Given multi-currency is one of this app's seven founding principles, replaced the alias with real routes to the real controller rather than double-registering the same URI (avoiding the exact silent-last-registration-wins landmine already documented for Helpdesk's KB routes). Deleted confirmed fully-orphaned `Tag`/`Language` models and `SharedResourcePolicy` (zero consumers anywhere).
  - **Cross-cutting note**: the phantom `users.tenant_id`/`users.role` columns (real, migrated, never in `User::$fillable`, never populated by the real registration flow or `DemoSeeder`) were found live-exploited or silently-broken in 5 of these 10 modules alone (AI, Sales, Achats, Integration, Workflow) on top of the 3 modules already fixed in the immediately-preceding chantiers (Setup, Reporting, Strategy) — 8 modules total this session. Both columns remain in the schema (harmless dead columns now that every real reader has been repointed to `company_id`/`hasAnyRole()`) rather than being dropped, since dropping a column already shipped in a public migration is a heavier, separate decision than fixing the code that misread it.
- **Chantier 8.6/8.7 — final consolidation + documentation refonte**: after 8.1 through 8.5-light closed the per-module audit/remediation loop across all 27 modules, this pass (a) rebuilt `docs/03-MODULES/*.md` (all 27 files — two new sections added to every one, `## Contrôleurs` and `## Vues (Vue/Inertia)`, plus a refresh of every existing section against the real, post-Chantier-8 code rather than the stale pre-session narrative) and `docs/06-BASE-DE-DONNEES/` (replaced with 6 real files — `SCHEMA-GENERAL.md` plus 5 `INVENTAIRE-0*.md` module-group table inventories plus a rewritten `DEPENDANCES-MODULES.md` — generated from a from-scratch grep of every `Schema::create()` call across the repo, including the loop-based catch-all migration a literal-string-only grep would miss), and (b) closed 2 real code gaps the documentation audit itself surfaced (documentation work that finds a live bug still gets the bug fixed, not just footnoted):
  - **5 real Eloquent models had `$table` declarations with no migration anywhere in the repo at all** — a step worse than the stub-table phenomenon documented throughout this file, since these weren't even scaffolded. Fixed to `ProjectTimeLog::class` (no import needed — same `Modules\Projects\Models` namespace).

Not deleted in this pass since it wasn't touched by the audit that found it and deleting dead root-namespace `app/` code (as opposed to `Modules/*` code) is a different blast radius than this chantier's established module-scoped precedent; flagged here for a future targeted cleanup pass.
- **Chantier 10 (Accounting + CRM + Sales) re-verification pass** — a full 7-layer re-audit of these 3 modules found real gaps Chantier 8.1/8.1b/8.2/8.5-light had missed, despite covering the same modules.
  - **Accounting — 24 dead routes resolved, not just deleted wholesale**: for each, checked whether real functionality already existed elsewhere before deciding delete vs rewire. `TaxController::entries` (real `TaxEntry` model existed, zero list endpoint — built a real filtered/paginated `entries()`), `ReconciliationController::suggestMatches` (real scoring logic existed in `BankReconciliationService::autoMatch()`'s private `findBestMatch()`/`calculateMatchScore()` — extracted a new public `suggestMatches()` returning ranked candidates instead of just the single best auto-match, wired to a real controller method), `ExpensesController::{reject,byPeriod,approveReport}` (real service/model support existed — `approveReport()` delegates to the already-real `ExpenseService::approve()`; `reject()`/`byPeriod()` are straightforward real CRUD/filter methods) were all built out for real. `AdvancedAccountingController`'s 16 missing methods (XBRL export, intercompany-rule automation, consolidation-worksheet review, ML-matching-metrics) and `PerformanceOptimizationController`'s 8 (cache/query-analysis/memory-profiling internals) were confirmed via repo-wide grep to have genuinely zero backing anywhere — no model, service, or table for any of the 24 concepts, unlike the ones above — routes deleted, not the controllers' real methods (`auditTrail`/`consolidationReport`/etc. and `cacheStats`/`clearCache`/`slowQueries` stay routed). `PerformanceOptimizationController::indexHealth()` (real, but zero route) was wired up as a bonus. Removed the dead relations/call (the controller's own `outstandingItems()`/`exceptions()` endpoints already returned hardcoded empty arrays, so no observable behavior changed) — real bank-transaction matching lives on `BankTransaction`/`BankReconciliationService`, a fully separate, correctly-working subsystem from the `Reconciliation` model these dead relations lived on.
  - **`ExpensesController` had 5 more real, active bugs found while fixing the dead routes**: `index()`/`byCategory()`/`analytics()`/`byPeriod()` all queried a bare `date` column that has never existed on `acc_expenses` (the real column is `expense_date`) — every one of these was a guaranteed "Unknown column" SQL error, not hypothetical; fixed to `expense_date` throughout. `store()`'s validated payload used a `date` key while `Expense::$fillable` only recognizes `expense_date` — mass-assignment silently dropped the date on every real expense creation even before the query-side bug was fixed; mapped explicitly. `show()` called `$expense->load('lines')` — `Expense` has no `lines()` relation at all (that belongs to the separate `ExpenseReport` model) — fixed by dropping the dead eager-load.

  - **`CostEngineController::resolveTenantId()`**: confirmed the client-controlled `?tenant_id=` query-param fallback (default 1) — fixed to `$request->user()->company_id` with no fallback, matching the identical bug pattern fixed repeatedly elsewhere this session (Setup/Reporting/Strategy/AI/Sales/Achats/Integration/Workflow).
  - **CRM — `CRMServiceProvider` policy registration**: confirmed zero `registerPolicies()`/`Gate::policy()` call despite `CampaignPolicy`/`WorkflowPolicy` already being correctly written and correctly called via `authorize()` in `CampaignController`/`WorkflowBuilderController` — active breakage (every non-super-admin unconditionally 403'd on campaigns/workflows), not just an RBAC hole. Fixed with a new `registerPolicies()`, also registering 2 new policies built in the same pass (see below).
  - **CRM — `CallRecordingController`/`RevenueIntelligenceController` cross-tenant leak + RBAC hole**: confirmed both had zero `authorize()` calls and zero `module:CRM` route gate; `RevenueInsight`/`RevenueTrend`/`RevenueAnomaly` had no `company_id` column at all, and `CallRecording`'s existing `company_id` column was never filtered on in any query — any authenticated user of any company could read (and for call recordings, queue AI summarization of) every other company's data. Fixed with an additive `company_id` migration for the 3 revenue-intelligence tables, tenant filtering added to every query in both controllers, `module:CRM` added to both route groups, and 2 new thin policies (`RevenueInsightPolicy` — fans out over all 3 revenue-intelligence models, mirroring `CustomerServiceAIPolicy`'s one-policy-many-models precedent; `CallRecordingPolicy`), both registered in `CRMServiceProvider`. The new `crm.revenue-intelligence.*`/`crm.call-recording.*` permission strings are **not yet seeded** in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`'s `CRM_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` block (out of this chantier's scope, which was told not to touch that file) — both new policies fail closed (deny, not throw) via a defensive `PermissionDoesNotExist`-catching wrapper rather than let an unseeded permission 500 every request; flagged here for Phase B.
